--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report — 2D Compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor Instability

**1. Problem Statement**

Heavy fluid ($\rho=2.0$) lies below light fluid ($\rho=1.0$) with effective gravity acting in $+y$ — an unstable stratification ($\mathbf{g}\cdot\nabla\rho < 0$). A single-mode velocity perturbation seeds the instability. Objective: capture the full evolution — exponential linear growth → nonlinear bubble/spike roll-up → chaotic mixing — on $t\in[0,3]$, and produce `summary_all.png`.

---

**2. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D Compressible Euler with gravitational source:
$$
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t \rho + \partial_x(\rho u) + \partial_y(\rho v) &= 0,\\
\partial_t(\rho u) + \partial_x(\rho u^2 + p) + \partial_y(\rho u v) &= 0,\\
\partial_t(\rho v) + \partial_x(\rho u v) + \partial_y(\rho v^2 + p) &= \rho g,\\
\partial_t E + \partial_x\!\big(u(E+p)\big) + \partial_y\!\big(v(E+p)\big) &= \rho g v,
\end{aligned}
$$
with total energy $E = \dfrac{p}{\gamma-1} + \dfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)$, $\gamma = 1.4$, and $g = 1.0$ (source adds momentum in $+y$).

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $\quad t \in [0, 3.0]$.

* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x = 0$ and $x = 0.25$: **Periodic**.
  * $y = 0$ and $y = 1$: **Reflective / free-slip walls** (no-penetration: $v = 0$; tangential velocity unrestricted).

* **Initial Conditions** (every field, exact):
$$
\rho(x,y,0) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot \tfrac{1}{2}\Big(1 + \tanh\big(50(y - 0.5)\big)\Big)
$$
$$
p(x,y,0) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5\\ y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5 \end{cases}
\qquad\text{(continuous at } y=0.5,\ p=2.0\text{)}
$$
$$
u(x,y,0) = 0
$$
$$
v(x,y,0) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\,\cos(8\pi x)\,\big[\sin(\pi y)\big]^6, \qquad c(x,y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(y)}{\rho(y)}}
$$
$$
E(x,y,0) = \frac{p(y)}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\rho(y)\, v(x,y)^2
$$
Note: $\big[\sin(\pi y)\big]^6$ vanishes at both walls → IC is compatible with the no-penetration condition.

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g} = [0,\, +1.0]^T$; $\mu = 0$; thermal conductivity $k = 0$; $\rho_{heavy}=2.0$, $\rho_{light}=1.0$; interface at $y=0.5$ (smoothing $50$); perturbation amplitude $0.025$, wavenumber $8\pi$ (single mode, $\lambda = 0.25$).

* **Expected Physics:** Linear exponential growth of the interface mode ($t \lesssim 0.6$); nonlinear regime: light-fluid bubbles rise toward $y=0$, heavy-fluid spikes sink toward $y=1$ ($t \approx 0.6$–$2$), with mushroom roll-up and secondary Kelvin–Helmholtz instability on spike flanks; transition to chaotic mixing ($t \approx 2$–$3$). The interface is a sharp **contact discontinuity**; weak acoustic transients are generated by the slight hydrostatic mismatch inside the smoothed interface layer (standard for this benchmark).

---

**3. Strategic Methodology**

* **Exact solution feasible?** **No.** (i) MoC applies only to 1D scalar/simple-wave problems — here we have a 2D nonlinear *system* with multi-dimensional wave interaction, vorticity generation, and secondary KH instability; characteristics cannot be inverted to a single-valued solution. (ii) Separation of variables / Green's functions are inapplicable (nonlinear, non-periodic in $y$, discontinuous data). (iii) The late-time dynamics are chaotic (mixing) — fundamentally non-integrable. Ground truth must be high-fidelity numerical.
* **Selection: DGSEM 2D + time.** Fourier Spectral is rejected: the $y$-direction has reflective walls (non-periodic basis invalid) and the evolving contact discontinuity would cause Gibbs contamination. DGSEM natively handles wall BCs, supports local refinement to track the thin rolling interface, and integrates the gravitational source consistently with the hyperbolic fluxes (the IC is in hydrostatic balance, so only the physical perturbation drives the dynamics).

---

**4. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults. (No $N$, $K$, CFL, or time-stepper overrides.)

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis (Signal-to-Noise):**
  * $\rho$ — **SELECTED.** Density *defines* the heavy/light interface (contact discontinuity), the structural feature of interest. It remains sharp and spatially localized (interface, spikes, bubbles) for all $t$ → high SNR, refinement concentrated exactly where needed.
  * $(u, v)$ — **REJECTED.** After $t \approx 1.5$ the mixing layer becomes globally chaotic/turbulent; refining on velocity refines the entire domain — wasted resources.
  * $p$ — **REJECTED.** Pressure is continuous across a contact interface (smooth hydrostatic profile + weak acoustics) → no interface signature.
  * $E$ — **REJECTED.** Dominated by the smooth hydrostatic background; the interface signal is buried.
  * **Constraint:** AMR and shock-capturing indicators must operate on **Density $\rho$**.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments:** None (Advisor report is empty; template defaults supersede nothing).

---

**5. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection:** $\rho$ (primary driver — buoyancy and interface morphology) and $v$ (vertical velocity — bubble/spike motion and growth). These capture the dominant RTI dynamics; do not plot $u, p, E$.
* **Task:** Plot $\rho$ (row 1) and $v$ (row 2) at **4 distinct times**: $t = 0.5,\ 1.0,\ 2.0,\ 3.0$ (columns).
* **Layout:** Grid, 2 rows (fields) × 4 columns (time).
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all conservative variables) at `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth.
* **Constraints:**
  * Save as: `summary_all.png` (strictly).
  * **DISABLE COLORBARS** (`colorbar=false`) — Julia.
  * No fixed colormaps or color limits; let scales adjust automatically per snapshot.
  * Plot only primary variables $\rho$, $v$ — no derived quantities (no schlieren, no vorticity).

---

**6. Cross-Validation & Data Integration**

* **Trigger check:** No external data paths (sensors / reference solutions) provided in the problem statement → **Not applicable**.

---

**REQUIRED COMPONENTS**

**Main Method:**
Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time

**Enhancements:**
* Adaptive Mesh Refinement (Density-based indicator)
* Shock Capturing (Density-based indicator)

#

Cell 3: Initial Conditions and Source Terms
- Action: Replace the lid-driven cavity initial state with the prescribed Rayleigh–Taylor density profile, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, and single-mode vertical-velocity perturbation.

- New Code (lines 1-36):
```julia
# ## Problem Setup: Rayleigh–Taylor Instability
#
# Heavy fluid ($\rho=2$) initially lies below light fluid ($\rho=1$),
# with a smoothed interface at $y=0.5$. The piecewise pressure profile is
# continuous at the interface. A single-mode vertical-velocity perturbation
# seeds the instability and vanishes at both horizontal walls.
#
# Gravity acts in the $+y$ direction, contributing $\rho g$ to the
# vertical-momentum equation and $\rho g v$ to the energy equation.

# %%
@inline function initial_condition_rti(x, t, equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    x_coord, y = x

    # Smoothed heavy-to-light density transition
    rho = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0) * 0.5 *
          (1.0 + tanh(50.0 * (y - 0.5)))

    # Continuous piecewise hydrostatic pressure
    p = y < 0.5 ? 2.0 * y + 1.0 : y + 1.5

    # Single-mode velocity perturbation
    v1 = 0.0
    sound_speed = sqrt(equations.gamma * p / rho)
    v2 = -0.025 * sound_speed * cos(8.0 * pi * x_coord) *
         sin(pi * y)^6

    # prim2cons gives E = p/(gamma-1) + rho*(v1^2+v2^2)/2
    return prim2cons(SVector(rho, v1, v2, p), equations)
end

@inline function source_terms_gravity(u, x, t,
                                      equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    g = 1.0
    rho, rho_v1, rho_v2, rho_e = u

    # Energy source: rho*g*v2 = g*rho_v2
    return SVector(0.0, 0.0, g * rho, g * rho_v2)
end

# Density variable used by both AMR and shock-capturing indicators
@inline function density_indicator(u, equations)
    return u[1]
end
```

```julia
# ## Boundary Conditions and Hyperbolic Semidiscretization
#
# The mesh provides periodic connectivity in the x-direction. At y=0 and y=1,
# `boundary_condition_slip_wall` imposes reflective free-slip Euler walls:
# zero normal velocity with unrestricted tangential velocity.
#
# The semidiscretization contains only the compressible Euler operator and
# the gravitational source; no viscous boundary conditions or parabolic
# operator are required.

# %%
boundary_conditions = Dict(
    :y_neg => boundary_condition_slip_wall,
    :y_pos => boundary_condition_slip_wall
)

semi = SemidiscretizationHyperbolic(mesh, equations,
                                    initial_condition_rti, dg;
                                    boundary_conditions=boundary_conditions,
                                    source_terms=source_terms_gravity)
```

```julia
# Exports the complete conservative state
# $\mathbf{u} = [\rho, \rho v_1, \rho v_2, \rho E]^T$
# every `dt_save = 0.1` time units. `PlotData2D` reconstructs the DG solution
# on a structured grid, while `cons2cons` preserves all conservative variables.
# The saved coordinates and state also support the later density/velocity plots.

# %%
npz_out = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
mkpath(npz_out)

dt_save = 0.1

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # Preserve the full conservative solution state
    pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2cons)

    rho    = getindex.(pd.data, 1)
    rho_v1 = getindex.(pd.data, 2)
    rho_v2 = getindex.(pd.data, 3)
    rho_e  = getindex.(pd.data, 4)

    filename = joinpath(npz_out, Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))

    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => t,
                            "x" => pd.x,
                            "y" => pd.y,
                            "rho" => rho,
                            "rho_v1" => rho_v1,
                            "rho_v2" => rho_v2,
                            "rho_e" => rho_e))
    println("Saved: $(basename(filename))")
    return nothing
end

# Save at t=0 and subsequently at fixed physical-time intervals
save_npz_callback = PeriodicCallback(save_npz_file, dt_save;
                                     initial_affect=true,
                                     save_positions=(false, false))
```

Cell 12: Post-Processing & Temporal Diagnostics
- Action: Select the prescribed snapshots \(t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0\) and plot only density and vertical velocity in a \(2\times4\) layout, with density in row 1 and vertical velocity in row 2.

- New Code (lines 1-77):
```julia
# ## Rayleigh–Taylor Snapshot Visualization
#
# Displays density (row 1) and vertical velocity (row 2) at
# t = 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, and 3.0. Vertical velocity is recovered from the
# saved conservative variables as v = rho_v2/rho. Each panel uses automatic
# color scaling, with no fixed colormap, color limits, or colorbar.

# %%
println("Generating Rayleigh–Taylor summary plots...")

files = sort([joinpath(npz_out, f) for f in readdir(npz_out)
              if endswith(f, ".npz")])
isempty(files) && error("No NPZ solution snapshots were found.")

# Read snapshot times once, then select the file nearest each requested time
file_times = [npzread(file)["t"] for file in files]
target_times = (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
snapshots = []

for target_time in target_times
    idx = argmin(abs.(file_times .- target_time))
    isapprox(file_times[idx], target_time; atol=1e-6, rtol=0.0) ||
        error("No saved snapshot found at t = $target_time")
    push!(snapshots, npzread(files[idx]))
end

style = (
    seriestype = :scatter,
    markersize = 2.5,
    markerstrokewidth = 0,
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    axis = nothing,
    framestyle = :none,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

density_plots = []
velocity_plots = []

for data in snapshots
    t_val = data["t"]
    x = vec(data["x"])
    y = vec(data["y"])

    rho = vec(data["rho"])
    v = vec(data["rho_v2"]) ./ rho

    push!(density_plots,
          plot(x, y, zcolor=rho,
               title="ρ, t=$(round(t_val, digits=2))"; style...))

    push!(velocity_plots,
          plot(x, y, zcolor=v,
               title="v, t=$(round(t_val, digits=2))"; style...))
end

# Row-major ordering: four density panels, then four vertical-velocity panels
plots_array = vcat(density_plots, velocity_plots)

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(2000, 1200), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))
println("Done.")
```

Inspector summary:
 The code implements the 2D compressible Euler Rayleigh-Taylor benchmark using DGSEM in Trixi. It sets gamma=1.4, domain [0,0.25]x[0,1], tspan [0,3], periodic boundaries in x and free-slip walls in y. The initial density, pressure, vertical velocity perturbation, and total energy are implemented exactly as specified, with gravity g=1.0 acting in +y through source terms. The spatial discretization uses polydeg=3, HLL surface flux, and hybrid DG-FV shock capturing with density-based IndicatorHennemannGassner. AMR uses a density-based IndicatorLöhner with ControllerThreeLevel. The code saves the full conservative state at dt_save=0.1 and produces summary_all.png containing rho and v at t=0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0 with no colorbars.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_PeriodicX_ReflectiveY_SmoothedDensityInterface\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

Create an appropiate Figure and saved it as summary_all.png

* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

Inspector summary:
 The code implements a 2D compressible Euler solver using Trixi's DGSEM with polydeg=3, HLL surface flux, and hybrid shock capturing using the Hennemann-Gassner indicator on density. AMR is configured with a Löhner indicator also based on density, with ControllerThreeLevel and AMRCallback. The domain is [0,0.25] x [0,1] with periodic x-boundaries and slip walls in y. Time integration uses RDPK3SpFSAL49 with dt=1e-5, adaptive=false, over t in [0,3]. The code attempts to save NPZ snapshots every 0.1 and plot density and vertical velocity at t=0.5,1,2,3.
Report: There are discrepancies with the base strategy:
The NPZ data saving is incorrect. In Trixi's PlotData2D, `pd.data` is a vector of variable fields (one array per conserved variable). The code uses `getindex.(pd.data, 1)`, `getindex.(pd.data, 2)`, etc., which extracts the i-th point from each variable field instead of the full field for each variable. As a result, the saved conservative variables are length-4 vectors rather than full spatial fields, so the required full solution state is not properly saved in the NPZ files and the subsequent plotting of density and vertical velocity cannot produce summary_all.png correctly. The strategy requires saving the full solution state for all conservative variables and producing summary_all.png; this logic is broken.

* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

- New Code (lines 12-31):
```julia
function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # PlotData2D stores each reconstructed point as a conservative state vector.
    pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2cons)
    states = pd.data

    # Extract variable fields rather than selecting individual state vectors.
    rho    = collect(getindex.(states, 1))
    rho_v1 = collect(getindex.(states, 2))
    rho_v2 = collect(getindex.(states, 3))
    rho_e  = collect(getindex.(states, 4))

    # NPZ requires ordinary numeric scalars and arrays.
    x = collect(pd.x)
    y = collect(pd.y)

    filename = joinpath(npz_out, Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))
    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => Float64(t),
                            "x" => x,
                            "y" => y,
                            "rho" => rho,
                            "rho_v1" => rho_v1,
                            "rho_v2" => rho_v2,
                            "rho_e" => rho_e))
    println("Saved: $(basename(filename))")
    return nothing
end
```

--- Advisor Markdown Report Received ---
## Section 1: Status Decision

* **Status:** **REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT**

The simulation completed successfully and displays the expected Rayleigh–Taylor morphology. However, it is not yet sufficiently verified or sharply resolved for PINN ground-truth generation. No previous trials exist, so neither the perfect-score nor history-saturation approval condition applies.

## Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard

* **Score Integrity:** **45/50**
* **Score Accuracy:** **17/20**
* **Score Details:** **10/15**
* **Score Optimality:** **13/15**
* **TOTAL SCORE:** **85/100**

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

**Execution and error-log check**

* The log ends with successful plot generation: `Saving summary_all.png...` followed by `Done.` There is no reported crash, `NaN`, or `Inf`.
* The supplied log tail contains **no** `Minimum achieved error:` entry.
* **Quantitative error is not applicable as no ground truth is available.** This is a forward nonlinear RTI simulation, so an error against zero or another artificial reference would not be meaningful.

**Physical evolution**

* At \(t=0.5\) and \(t=1.0\), the density perturbation remains localized around the initial heavy/light interface.
* By \(t=2.0\), a nonlinear plume with a mushroom-like cap has developed. At \(t=3.0\), elongated spikes/bubbles and rolled-up side structures are visible.
* The vertical-velocity snapshots are initially concentrated near the interface and subsequently align with the extending density structures. This is qualitatively consistent with unstable RT growth followed by nonlinear bubble/spike development.
* There is no obvious explosive oscillation, checkerboarding, or material crossing the horizontal walls. Nevertheless, the figure alone cannot verify wall-normal velocity, positivity, conservation, or quantitative growth rates.

**Resolution and visualization concerns**

* Regular horizontal white stripes appear throughout **both fields and all times**, including otherwise quiescent regions. Their regularity makes a plotting/AMR-patch stitching artifact—such as cell-edge strokes, gaps, or defective hanging-node visualization—much more likely than physical Gibbs oscillations. They substantially obscure the small-scale structures.
* The mushroom caps and density interfaces at \(t=2\) and \(t=3\) appear somewhat soft rather than razor-sharp. Fine Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up is therefore not yet convincingly resolved.

### Diagnosis

The principal numerical concern is **contact-wave dissipation**, not a stability failure:

* `flux_hll` is a two-wave flux that does not explicitly restore the contact/shear wave. It can excessively diffuse the density interface, which is precisely the feature of interest here.
* The Lax–Friedrichs FV subcell flux is also highly dissipative. Because the shock-capturing indicator operates on density, a steep but non-shocking RT contact may remain flagged and repeatedly receive FV blending.
* The current DGSEM/AMR framework is otherwise appropriate. `flux_ranocha` in smooth DG regions, density-based Löhner AMR, and adaptive Runge–Kutta time integration are reasonable choices.

An exact solution is **not feasible** for the full request: multidimensional nonlinear Euler with gravitational forcing, wall interactions, vorticity generation, and nonlinear RT/KH roll-up cannot be solved by separation of variables or invertible characteristics. Linear Fourier/RT analysis could verify only the early linear regime, not the solution through \(t=3\). Thus, no exact-method architecture override is warranted.

### Targeted Improvements

**For Integrity**

* Regenerate the figure using gapless, AMR-aware visualization: either sample the DG solution onto a uniform visualization grid or correctly stitch non-conforming patches. Disable element-edge strokes/gaps and verify interpolation across hanging nodes. Do **not** hide the stripes by smoothing the physical data.
* Add explicit log checks for successful arrival at \(t=3\), finite fields, and positive \(\rho\) and \(p\). No CFL reduction is presently indicated by the logs.

**For Accuracy**

* After isolating the flux/plotting changes, perform a **`polydeg=5`** fidelity run while retaining the present AMR limits and adaptive time stepping. This should improve smooth vortical roll-up without immediately increasing the AMR maximum level; expect a smaller stable time step.
* Compare the \(p=3\) and \(p=5\) results on a common output grid using interface/spike positions, mixing-layer width, kinetic energy, and field differences at the saved times. A single visually plausible run is not sufficient certification for PINN ground truth.
* Confirm that the full conservative state \((\rho,\rho u,\rho v,E)\), times, and mesh metadata are actually saved at `dt_save=0.1`; the supplied log confirms only the PNG.

**For Details**

* For the next refinement, replace **`flux_hll` with `flux_hllc`** while initially keeping AMR and polynomial degree fixed, so the improvement in contact resolution can be attributed cleanly.
* If the density indicator keeps FV subcells active over most of the contact, modestly reduce shock-capturing aggressiveness, such as `alpha_max`, while retaining local FV blending for positivity and genuine acoustic shocks. If ringing appears, restore blending locally rather than returning to globally dissipative HLL.
* Do not increase the AMR maximum level yet; first remove avoidable contact dissipation and fix the visualization.

**For Optimality**

* Retain DGSEM, density-based AMR, `flux_ranocha`, and `RDPK3SpFSAL49`. The main methodological upgrade needed is contact-resolving surface dissipation, followed by polynomial-degree convergence verification.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report — Trial #8 (Refinement)
**Problem:** 2D Compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor Instability | **Prior:** Run #7 (85/100, REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT)

---

**1. Problem Statement**

Refinement of the inviscid compressible Euler RTI simulation. The Run #7 DGSEM/AMR architecture is physically sound (correct morphology, no NaN/blowup). The Advisor's forensics identify three fixable deficiencies, none of which justify an architecture change: (i) **contact-wave dissipation** from `flux_hll` softening the RT interface (the feature of interest), (ii) **striped AMR-stitching visualization artifacts** masquerading as oscillations, and (iii) **missing quantitative verification and data archival**. Strategy: retain the DGSEM+AMR skeleton, upgrade to a contact-resolving HLLC surface flux, raise `polydeg` 3→5, repair the visualization pipeline, and certify the ground truth via a p=3/p=5 convergence comparison.

---

**1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D compressible Euler with gravitational source ($g = 1.0$, acting in $+y$):

$$\partial_t \begin{pmatrix} \rho \\ \rho u \\ \rho v \\ E \end{pmatrix} + \partial_x \begin{pmatrix} \rho u \\ \rho u^2 + p \\ \rho u v \\ u(E+p) \end{pmatrix} + \partial_y \begin{pmatrix} \rho v \\ \rho u v \\ \rho v^2 + p \\ v(E+p) \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \rho g \\ \rho v g \end{pmatrix}$$

Equation of state: $p = (\gamma - 1)\left(E - \tfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2 + v^2)\right)$, $\gamma = 1.4$. Inviscid: $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$.

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $t \in [0, 3.0]$.
* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x = 0$ and $x = 0.25$: **Periodic**.
  * $y = 0$ and $y = 1$: **Reflective / free-slip walls** — no-penetration $v = 0$; mirror state $(\rho, \rho u, \rho v, E) \mapsto (\rho, \rho u, -\rho v, E)$.

* **Initial Conditions** (interface at $y_0 = 0.5$):
$$\rho(y) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left[1 + \tanh\left(50\,(y - 0.5)\right)\right]$$
$$p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5 \end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y) = 0$$
$$v(x,y) = -0.025\, c(y)\, \cos(8\pi x)\, \sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c(y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(y)}{\rho(y)}}$$
$$E(x,y) = \frac{p(y)}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\rho(y)\, v(x,y)^2$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g} = [0, +1.0]^T$; $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$, $\rho_{light} = 1.0$; interface $y_0 = 0.5$; tanh steepness $50$; perturbation amplitude $0.025$; perturbation wavenumber $8\pi$ (single mode over the $x$-period $0.25$); $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$.
* **Expected Physics:** Piecewise hydrostatic balance $\partial_y p = \rho g$ holds initially; dynamics driven by the single-mode perturbation. Linear RT growth ($t \lesssim 1$) → nonlinear plumes with mushroom caps ($t \approx 1.5$–$2$) → KH roll-up on plume flanks and secondary instabilities ($t \approx 2$–$3$). Heavy-fluid spikes penetrate in $+y$; light-fluid bubbles penetrate in $-y$. The interface is a **contact discontinuity** (the certification target); only weak acoustic transients are expected. Positivity of $\rho, p$ must hold through $t = 3$.

---

**2. Strategic Methodology**

* **Exact solution feasible? NO.** (i) MoC requires invertible 1D characteristic relations; this problem is 2D, gravitationally forced, and its late-time dynamics are vortical (RT/KH roll-up) — characteristics neither decouple nor close, and no shock-root selection problem exists to exploit. (ii) Separation of variables / Green's functions fail: nonlinear convective fluxes, non-periodic $y$, inhomogeneous source. (iii) Linear RT stability theory covers only the early growth regime, not $t \le 3$. The Advisor concurs: no exact-method override is warranted.
* **Selection: Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time** (retained). It is the only toolbox method delivering simultaneously: high-order accuracy for the smooth vortical roll-up, subcell FV blending for positivity at the steep contact, and AMR to track the moving interface. Run #7's failures were flux- and post-processing-level, not method-level.
* **AMR variable (Signal-to-Noise Analysis):** **Density $\rho$.** The interface, spikes, and bubbles are *density structures*: $\rho$ is sharp exactly at the features of interest and quiescent (constant 2.0 / 1.0) elsewhere — maximal signal-to-noise. Velocity becomes globally chaotic across the entire mixing layer (refining on it wastes resources on turbulence, not structure); pressure is dominated by the smooth hydrostatic profile $p(y)$ and does not localize the interface. Density-based indication is superior for this PDE and retained.

---

**3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults **except** the Advisor-mandated overrides in §3.1. Do NOT alter mesh, AMR limits, CFL, or time stepper beyond what is listed.

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing:**
  * *Indicator variable:* $\rho$ for both the Löhner AMR indicator and the Hennemann–Gassner shock-capturing indicator (justified in §2).
  * *AMR:* retain `IndicatorLohner`($\rho$) with `ControllerThreeLevel` (base=4, med=5, max=6). Do **not** raise the maximum level.
  * *Shock capturing:* retain `VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG` with `flux_lax_friedrichs` FV subcells; **modestly reduce `alpha_max` (e.g., 1.0 → 0.5)** so the steep-but-non-shocking RT contact is not perpetually blended to FV. If ringing appears, restore blending locally (raise `alpha_max`) — do **not** revert to globally dissipative HLL.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides):**
  1. **Surface flux:** `flux_hllc` (replaces `flux_hll`) for contact resolution. Volume flux `flux_ranocha` retained.
  2. **Primary run:** `polydeg=5`, retaining AMR limits and adaptive stepping (`RDPK3SpFSAL49`, initial dt = 1e-5). A smaller stable step is expected — the adaptive controller handles it; **no CFL reduction**.
  3. **Companion verification run:** `polydeg=3` with otherwise identical settings (HLLC, same AMR) to isolate the flux change and enable p-convergence certification.
  4. **Data archival (mandatory):** save the full conservative state $(\rho, \rho u, \rho v, E)$, times, and mesh/AMR metadata every `dt_save = 0.1`. (Run #7 saved only the PNG.)
  5. **Log checks (mandatory prints):** successful arrival at $t = 3.0$; all fields finite (no NaN/Inf); $\min \rho > 0$; $\min p > 0$.
  6. **Visualization repair:** gapless, AMR-aware rendering — at each plot time, interpolate the DG/AMR solution onto a common **uniform visualization grid** (square cells, ≥ 4 points per finest AMR element edge, e.g., $512 \times 2048$), sampling each point from the leaf element containing it (hanging-node correct). Render one gapless heatmap per field; disable element-edge strokes/gaps. Do **not** smooth the physical data to hide stripes.

---

**4. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection (2):** $\rho$ — primary driver (interface, spikes, bubbles, mixing layer); $v = \rho v / \rho$ — vertical velocity (plume dynamics). Omit $u, p, E$ from the figure.
* **Times:** $t = 0.5,\ 1.0,\ 2.0,\ 3.0$.
* **Layout:** 2 rows (fields) × 4 columns (times), rendered from the uniform gapless grid of §3.1(6). Figure generated from the **p=5 primary run**.
* **Julia:** `colorbar=false` (mandatory). No fixed colormaps — auto-scaled per field/time.
* **Save as:** `summary_all.png` (strict).
* **Data:** full-state saving at `dt_save = 0.1` proceeds independently of the figure.

---

**5. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**

* No external reference data paths in [USER_QUESTION] → no external $L_2$ comparison.
* **Internal cross-validation (Advisor-mandated):** interpolate the p=3 and p=5 runs onto a common uniform output grid at the saved times ($t = 1.0, 2.0, 3.0$) and **print**:
  * Spike/bubble tip positions ($y$-extremes of the $\rho = 1.5$ contour);
  * Mixing-layer width (tip-to-tip separation, or $y$-extent of $1.1 < \rho < 1.9$);
  * Kinetic energy $\int_\Omega \tfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)\,dV$ vs. time for both runs;
  * Relative $L_2$ differences of $\rho$ and $v$ between p=3 and p=5 at each saved time.
* The **p=5 run is the certified ground truth**; these metrics constitute the certification. A single visually plausible run is insufficient.

---

**REQUIRED COMPONENTS**

**Main Method:**
* Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time

**Enhancements:**

```julia
# ## Strategy: Fixed-Time Conservative-State and AMR Serialization (.npz)
#
# Exports the complete conservative state
# $\mathbf{u} = [\rho, \rho v_1, \rho v_2, \rho E]^T$
# every `dt_save = 0.1` time units. In addition to the reconstructed fields,
# each file contains the raw DG state and leaf-element coordinates, bounds,
# and AMR levels. These metadata permit reconstruction on the actual adaptive
# mesh without assuming a conforming structured grid. Separate output
# directories prevent the p=5 and p=3 runs from overwriting each other.
# %%
npz_root = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
dt_save = 0.1

function make_npz_callbacks(output_directory, run_polydeg)
    mkpath(output_directory)

    function save_snapshot(integrator)
        semi = integrator.p
        t = integrator.t

        # Retain reconstructed fields for interoperability and quick inspection.
        pd = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi, solution_variables=cons2cons)
        states = pd.data
        rho    = collect(getindex.(states, 1))
        rho_v1 = collect(getindex.(states, 2))
        rho_v2 = collect(getindex.(states, 3))
        rho_e  = collect(getindex.(states, 4))
        x = collect(pd.x)
        y = collect(pd.y)

        # Raw layout: (variable, node_x, node_y, leaf_element).
        u_cons = copy(Trixi.wrap_array(integrator.u, semi))
        node_coordinates = copy(semi.cache.elements.node_coordinates)
        n_elements = size(u_cons, 4)
        @assert size(node_coordinates, 4) == n_elements

        domain_min = [Float64(coordinates_min[1]),
                      Float64(coordinates_min[2])]
        domain_max = [Float64(coordinates_max[1]),
                      Float64(coordinates_max[2])]
        domain_size = domain_max .- domain_min

        # Bounds rows are xmin, xmax, ymin, ymax. Since each p4est refinement
        # halves both element dimensions, the bounds also encode the AMR level.
        element_bounds = Matrix{Float64}(undef, 4, n_elements)
        element_levels = Vector{Int}(undef, n_elements)
        for element in 1:n_elements
            xs = view(node_coordinates, 1, :, :, element)
            ys = view(node_coordinates, 2, :, :, element)
            element_bounds[1, element] = minimum(xs)
            element_bounds[2, element] = maximum(xs)
            element_bounds[3, element] = minimum(ys)
            element_bounds[4, element] = maximum(ys)

            dx = element_bounds[2, element] - element_bounds[1, element]
            dy = element_bounds[4, element] - element_bounds[3, element]
            level_estimate = 0.5 * (log2(domain_size[1] / dx) +
                                    log2(domain_size[2] / dy))
            element_levels[element] = round(Int, level_estimate)
        end

        filename = joinpath(output_directory,
                            Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))
        npzwrite(filename, Dict{String, Any}(
            "t" => Float64(t),
            "polydeg" => Int(run_polydeg),
            "x" => x,
            "y" => y,
            "rho" => rho,
            "rho_v1" => rho_v1,
            "rho_v2" => rho_v2,
            "rho_e" => rho_e,
            "u_cons" => u_cons,
            "leaf_node_coordinates" => node_coordinates,
            "leaf_element_bounds" => element_bounds,
            "element_levels" => element_levels,
            "element_ids" => collect(1:n_elements),
            "n_elements" => n_elements,
            "n_nodes_1d" => size(u_cons, 2),
            "domain_min" => domain_min,
            "domain_max" => domain_max,
            "periodicity" => [1, 0],
            "amr_controller_levels" => [4, 5, 6],
            "amr_interval" => 20,
            "amr_indicator_component" => 1,
            "shock_indicator_component" => 1))
        println("Saved: $(filename)")
        return nothing
    end

    save_callback = PeriodicCallback(save_snapshot, dt_save;
                                     initial_affect=true,
                                     save_positions=(false, false))
    final_callback = PresetTimeCallback([3.0], save_snapshot;
                                        save_positions=(false, false))
    return save_callback, final_callback
end

# Primary p=5 archive; the companion run constructs its own p=3 callbacks.
npz_out = joinpath(npz_root, "p$(polydeg)")
save_npz_callback, save_final_npz_callback = make_npz_callbacks(npz_out, polydeg)
```

```julia
function log_run_checks(label, run_sol, run_semi)
    final_time = run_sol.t[end]
    reached_t_end = isapprox(final_time, 3.0; atol=1.0e-10, rtol=0.0)

    # Raw layout: (variable, node_x, node_y, leaf_element).
    u_cons = Trixi.wrap_array(run_sol.u[end], run_semi)
    fields_finite = all(isfinite, u_cons)

    min_rho = Inf
    min_pressure = Inf
    gamma = run_semi.equations.gamma

    for element in axes(u_cons, 4), j in axes(u_cons, 3), i in axes(u_cons, 2)
        rho    = u_cons[1, i, j, element]
        rho_v1 = u_cons[2, i, j, element]
        rho_v2 = u_cons[3, i, j, element]
        rho_e  = u_cons[4, i, j, element]

        pressure = (gamma - 1) *
                   (rho_e - 0.5 * (rho_v1^2 + rho_v2^2) / rho)
        min_rho = min(min_rho, rho)
        min_pressure = min(min_pressure, pressure)
    end

    println("[$label] successful arrival at t = 3.0: ", reached_t_end,
            " (final t = ", final_time, ", retcode = ", run_sol.retcode, ")")
    println("[$label] all fields finite (no NaN/Inf): ", fields_finite)
    println("[$label] minimum rho = ", min_rho,
            "; min rho > 0: ", min_rho > 0)
    println("[$label] minimum p = ", min_pressure,
            "; min p > 0: ", min_pressure > 0)

    return (reached_t_end=reached_t_end,
            fields_finite=fields_finite,
            min_rho=min_rho,
            min_pressure=min_pressure)
end

println("Starting p=5 primary RTI run...")
```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 11: Data Regularization (Rasterization) ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
Cell 11: Data Regularization / AMR-Aware Interpolation
- Action: Replace nearest-neighbor binning with tensor-product Lagrange interpolation of the archived DG state. Every output point is sampled only from the leaf element containing it, so differently refined neighboring elements require no conforming-grid assumption.
- Action: Use a default `512×2048` cell-centered grid over `[0,0.25]×[0,1]`; its cells are square and provide at least eight samples across a finest-level element edge.
- Action: Return all four conservative variables together with density, vertical velocity, and grid spacing for subsequent visualization and verification. No smoothing or scatter-based rendering is introduced.
- New Code (lines 1-44):
```julia
# ## Methodology: Leaf-Element DG Interpolation on a Uniform Grid
#
# Each archived leaf element owns a tensor-product LGL polynomial. For every
# uniform-grid point, the containing leaf element is identified from its
# physical bounds and the conservative state is evaluated by two successive
# one-dimensional Lagrange interpolations. No neighbor averaging, smoothing,
# or nearest-neighbor splatting is used. Consequently, hanging nodes and
# element-edge strokes cannot introduce gaps or stripes in the sampled data.
#
# The default 512 x 2048 grid has square cells on [0, 0.25] x [0, 1].
# Cell centers avoid ambiguous sampling exactly on element interfaces.
# %%
function lagrange_interpolation_matrix(nodes, points)
    n = length(nodes)
    weights = Matrix{Float64}(undef, n, length(points))

    for (point_index, point) in enumerate(points)
        for a in 1:n
            weight = 1.0
            for b in 1:n
                if b != a
                    weight *= (point - nodes[b]) / (nodes[a] - nodes[b])
                end
            end
            weights[a, point_index] = weight
        end
    end

    return weights
end

function interpolate_amr_snapshot(data; nx=512, ny=2048)
    u_cons = data["u_cons"]
    node_coordinates = data["leaf_node_coordinates"]
    element_bounds = data["leaf_element_bounds"]
    domain_min = data["domain_min"]
    domain_max = data["domain_max"]

    @assert size(u_cons, 1) == 4 "Expected four conservative variables."
    @assert size(u_cons, 2) == size(u_cons, 3) "Expected a tensor-product DG element."

    n = size(u_cons, 2)
    n_elements = size(u_cons, 4)
    @assert size(node_coordinates, 4) == n_elements
    @assert size(element_bounds, 2) == n_elements

    x_min, y_min = Float64(domain_min[1]), Float64(domain_min[2])
    x_max, y_max = Float64(domain_max[1]), Float64(domain_max[2])
    dx = (x_max - x_min) / nx
    dy = (y_max - y_min) / ny
    isapprox(dx, dy; atol=1.0e-14, rtol=1.0e-12) ||
        error("The common visualization grid must use square cells.")

    # Cell-centered coordinates permit gapless heatmap rendering.
    x_grid = [x_min + (i - 0.5) * dx for i in 1:nx]
    y_grid = [y_min + (j - 0.5) * dy for j in 1:ny]

    # Layout: (y, x, conservative variable).
    u_grid = Array{Float64}(undef, ny, nx, 4)
    fill!(u_grid, NaN)
    covered = falses(ny, nx)

    for element in 1:n_elements
        # LGL coordinates of this leaf element in physical space.
        x_nodes = collect(view(node_coordinates, 1, :, 1, element))
        y_nodes = collect(view(node_coordinates, 2, 1, :, element))

        # Only grid points inside this leaf are evaluated from its polynomial.
        i_first = searchsortedfirst(x_grid, element_bounds[1, element])
        i_last  = searchsortedlast(x_grid, element_bounds[2, element])
        j_first = searchsortedfirst(y_grid, element_bounds[3, element])
        j_last  = searchsortedlast(y_grid, element_bounds[4, element])

        if i_first > i_last || j_first > j_last
            continue
        end

        i_range = i_first:i_last
        j_range = j_first:j_last
        nx_local = length(i_range)
        ny_local = length(j_range)

        wx = lagrange_interpolation_matrix(x_nodes, view(x_grid, i_range))
        wy = lagrange_interpolation_matrix(y_nodes, view(y_grid, j_range))

        # First interpolate in x for each retained y-node. Layout of temporary:
        # (y-node, local x-point, conservative variable).
        temporary = Array{Float64}(undef, n, nx_local, 4)
        for variable in 1:4, b in 1:n, local_i in 1:nx_local
            value = 0.0
            for a in 1:n
                value += wx[a, local_i] * u_cons[variable, a, b, element]
            end
            temporary[b, local_i, variable] = value
        end

        # Complete the tensor-product interpolation in y.
        for variable in 1:4, local_i in 1:nx_local, local_j in 1:ny_local
            value = 0.0
            for b in 1:n
                value += wy[b, local_j] * temporary[b, local_i, variable]
            end

            i = i_range[local_i]
            j = j_range[local_j]
            u_grid[j, i, variable] = value
        end

        for local_j in 1:ny_local, local_i in 1:nx_local
            covered[j_range[local_j], i_range[local_i]] = true
        end
    end

    all(covered) ||
        error("Leaf-element reconstruction left uncovered uniform-grid points.")

    rho = view(u_grid, :, :, 1)
    vertical_velocity = u_grid[:, :, 3] ./ rho

    return (x=x_grid,
            y=y_grid,
            u=u_grid,
            rho=rho,
            v=vertical_velocity,
            dx=dx,
            dy=dy,
            cell_area=dx * dy)
end
```

Cell 12: Post-Processing, Visualization & Verification

- New Code (lines 1-68):
```julia
# ## Rayleigh–Taylor Visualization and Polynomial-Degree Certification
#
# Density and vertical velocity are rendered as gapless heatmaps from the
# p=5 run at t = 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, and 3.0. The p=3 and p=5 archives are also
# compared on the same uniform grid at t = 1.0, 2.0, and 3.0. No smoothing,
# fixed colormap, fixed color limits, or artificial reference error is used.
# %%
function snapshot_time(data)
    value = data["t"]
    return value isa Number ? Float64(value) : Float64(only(value))
end

function snapshot_at_time(directory, target_time)
    files = sort([joinpath(directory, f) for f in readdir(directory)
                  if endswith(f, ".npz")])
    isempty(files) && error("No NPZ solution snapshots were found in $directory.")

    file_times = [snapshot_time(npzread(file)) for file in files]
    idx = argmin(abs.(file_times .- target_time))
    isapprox(file_times[idx], target_time; atol=1.0e-6, rtol=0.0) ||
        error("No saved snapshot found at t = $target_time in $directory.")

    return npzread(files[idx])
end

# Find the y-extremes of the rho=1.5 interface by linearly locating its
# crossings along every uniform-grid x-column.
function interface_metrics(grid)
    rho = grid.rho
    y = grid.y
    crossings = Float64[]

    for i in axes(rho, 2)
        for j in 1:(size(rho, 1) - 1)
            r_lower = rho[j, i]
            r_upper = rho[j + 1, i]
            if !(isfinite(r_lower) && isfinite(r_upper))
                continue
            end

            d_lower = r_lower - 1.5
            d_upper = r_upper - 1.5

            if d_lower == 0.0
                push!(crossings, y[j])
            end

            if d_lower * d_upper < 0.0
                fraction = (1.5 - r_lower) / (r_upper - r_lower)
                push!(crossings,
                      y[j] + fraction * (y[j + 1] - y[j]))
            end
        end

        if isfinite(rho[end, i]) && rho[end, i] == 1.5
            push!(crossings, y[end])
        end
    end

    if isempty(crossings)
        return (bubble_tip=NaN, spike_tip=NaN, mixing_width=NaN)
    end

    bubble_tip = minimum(crossings)
    spike_tip = maximum(crossings)
    return (bubble_tip=bubble_tip,
            spike_tip=spike_tip,
            mixing_width=spike_tip - bubble_tip)
end

function relative_l2_difference(field, reference)
    numerator = sqrt(sum(abs2, field .- reference))
    denominator = sqrt(sum(abs2, reference))
    return denominator > 0.0 ? numerator / denominator : NaN
end

function uniform_grid_kinetic_energy(grid)
    rho = grid.u[:, :, 1]
    rho_v1 = grid.u[:, :, 2]
    rho_v2 = grid.u[:, :, 3]

    return grid.cell_area *
           sum((rho_v1.^2 .+ rho_v2.^2) ./ (2.0 .* rho))
end

# Integrate kinetic energy directly on each archived leaf element using its
# LGL quadrature weights and affine physical Jacobian.
function archive_kinetic_energy(data)
    u = data["u_cons"]
    bounds = data["leaf_element_bounds"]
    n = size(u, 2)
    weights = LobattoLegendreBasis(n - 1).weights
    energy = 0.0

    for element in axes(u, 4)
        jacobian = ((bounds[2, element] - bounds[1, element]) / 2.0) *
                   ((bounds[4, element] - bounds[3, element]) / 2.0)

        for j in 1:n, i in 1:n
            rho = u[1, i, j, element]
            kinetic_density =
                (u[2, i, j, element]^2 + u[3, i, j, element]^2) /
                (2.0 * rho)
            energy += kinetic_density * weights[i] * weights[j] * jacobian
        end
    end

    return energy
end

function kinetic_energy_history(directory)
    files = sort([joinpath(directory, f) for f in readdir(directory)
                  if endswith(f, ".npz")])
    times = Float64[]
    energies = Float64[]

    for file in files
        data = npzread(file)
        push!(times, snapshot_time(data))
        push!(energies, archive_kinetic_energy(data))
    end

    return times, energies
end

println("Generating Rayleigh–Taylor summary plots...")

target_times = (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
primary_grids = [
    interpolate_amr_snapshot(snapshot_at_time(npz_out, target_time))
    for target_time in target_times
]

style = (
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    axis = nothing,
    framestyle = :none,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

density_plots = []
velocity_plots = []

for (target_time, grid) in zip(target_times, primary_grids)
    # Heatmaps use contiguous cells and therefore have no element-edge
    # strokes or AMR-patch gaps. Each panel receives automatic color scaling.
    push!(density_plots,
          heatmap(grid.x, grid.y, grid.rho;
                  title="ρ, t=$(round(target_time, digits=2))",
                  style...))

    push!(velocity_plots,
          heatmap(grid.x, grid.y, grid.v;
                  title="v, t=$(round(target_time, digits=2))",
                  style...))
end

# Row-major ordering: four density panels, then four velocity panels.
plots_array = vcat(density_plots, velocity_plots)

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(2000, 1200), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))

npz_out_p3 = joinpath(npz_root, "p3")

println("Kinetic energy versus time for both archived runs:")
for (label, directory) in (("p=3", npz_out_p3), ("p=5", npz_out))
    times, energies = kinetic_energy_history(directory)
    println("  $label:")
    for (time, energy) in zip(times, energies)
        Printf.@printf("    t = %.2f, KE = %.12e\n", time, energy)
    end
end

println("Common-grid p=3/p=5 certification; p=5 is the reference:")
for target_time in (1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
    primary_index = findfirst(
        time -> isapprox(time, target_time; atol=1.0e-6, rtol=0.0),
        target_times)
    primary_index === nothing &&
        error("Primary grid is missing t = $target_time.")

    grid_p5 = primary_grids[primary_index]
    grid_p3 = interpolate_amr_snapshot(
        snapshot_at_time(npz_out_p3, target_time))

    metrics_p3 = interface_metrics(grid_p3)
    metrics_p5 = interface_metrics(grid_p5)
    ke_p3 = uniform_grid_kinetic_energy(grid_p3)
    ke_p5 = uniform_grid_kinetic_energy(grid_p5)
    l2_rho = relative_l2_difference(grid_p3.rho, grid_p5.rho)
    l2_v = relative_l2_difference(grid_p3.v, grid_p5.v)

    Printf.@printf("\nt = %.1f\n", target_time)
    Printf.@printf(
        "  p=3: bubble tip y = %.8f, spike tip y = %.8f, " *
        "mixing width = %.8f, KE = %.12e\n",
        metrics_p3.bubble_tip, metrics_p3.spike_tip,
        metrics_p3.mixing_width, ke_p3)
    Printf.@printf(
        "  p=5: bubble tip y = %.8f, spike tip y = %.8f, " *
        "mixing width = %.8f, KE = %.12e\n",
        metrics_p5.bubble_tip, metrics_p5.spike_tip,
        metrics_p5.mixing_width, ke_p5)
    Printf.@printf(
        "  relative L2 differences versus p=5: rho = %.8e, v = %.8e\n",
        l2_rho, l2_v)
end

println("Done.")
```

Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_PeriodicX_ReflectiveY_SmoothedDensityInterface\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

## Section 1: Status Decision

**Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

Trial #8 is a substantial improvement over Run #7: it is stable, visually clean, and physically credible. However, the p=3/p=5 comparison does **not** yet certify the p=5 result as high-fidelity ground truth, particularly for the late-time velocity and fine-scale roll-up.

The saturation rule does not apply: the only previous trial, Run #7, scored **85**, so there is no earlier score ≥90 from which to count five refinement trials.

## Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard

- **Score Integrity:** 50/50
- **Score Accuracy:** 18/20
- **Score Details:** 12/15
- **Score Optimality:** 15/15
- **TOTAL SCORE:** **95/100**

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

**Execution and integrity**

- The log reaches \(t=3.00\), reports a final kinetic energy of approximately \(1.63945\times10^{-2}\), and ends with `Done.`
- The kinetic-energy history increases smoothly through the late-time interval, with no evidence of a numerical blow-up.
- All eight panels are populated and readable. The previous AMR-stitching/striping problem is no longer apparent.
- There are no visible NaN/Inf artifacts, blank panels, or catastrophic positivity failures. The numerical values of the minimum density and pressure checks are not included in the supplied tail, so they should remain explicitly visible in future final log summaries.

**Error-log check**

No `Minimum achieved error:` line appears in the supplied stdout tail.

**Quantitative error is not applicable as no ground truth is available.** This is a nonlinear forward RTI simulation without an analytical reference. The reported p=3/p=5 relative \(L_2\) values are **resolution-difference diagnostics**, not errors against an exact solution.

**Physical behavior**

The density sequence shows the expected progression:

1. **\(t=0.5\):** a perturbed, still-thin heavy/light interface.
2. **\(t=1.0\):** a coherent central heavy-fluid protrusion in \(+y\).
3. **\(t=2.0\):** a mushroom-shaped cap with rolled flanks.
4. **\(t=3.0\):** an elongated spike and complicated secondary Kelvin–Helmholtz-like filaments.

This is consistent with the prescribed upward body force and inverted configuration: heavy-fluid spikes penetrate in \(+y\), while light-fluid bubbles penetrate in \(-y\). The vertical-velocity panels consistently localize the strongest motion around the plume and mixing layer. At \(t=3\), the p=5 spike and bubble tips remain inside the domain, so there is no visible wall-penetration problem.

The large-scale morphology is approximately symmetric during the early coherent growth stage, and there is no obvious Gibbs ringing. Increasing kinetic energy is physically compatible with gravitational potential energy being converted into fluid motion; total energy conservation is not expected in the presence of the gravitational source.

**Resolution certification**

The p=3/p=5 differences grow substantially with time:

| Time | Relative \(\rho\) difference | Relative \(v\) difference |
|---|---:|---:|
| \(t=1\) | 0.0162 | 0.0757 |
| \(t=2\) | 0.0599 | 0.1379 |
| \(t=3\) | 0.1589 | 0.2585 |

The integral and large-scale measures are less sensitive:

- At \(t=3\), mixing-layer widths are 0.72372 and 0.74257, a difference of about **2.5%**.
- The corresponding kinetic energies differ by about **4.9%**.
- The spike tips are close, but the bubble tips differ by approximately **0.0226** in \(y\), which is significant relative to the plume displacement.

Thus, the large-scale growth is reasonably consistent, but the pointwise late-time fields—especially velocity and the rolled-up filaments—remain resolution-sensitive. Two polynomial degrees are also insufficient to establish a convergence trend. The p=5 run can serve as the better current reference, but it should not yet be described as certified or exact ground truth.

### Diagnosis

**Primary diagnosis:** resolution-sensitive nonlinear RT/KH mixing and remaining discretization uncertainty, **not** a stability failure.

The HLLC flux, higher polynomial degree, and repaired visualization have addressed the major Run #7 deficiencies. The remaining late-time discrepancies are plausibly caused by a combination of:

- different numerical dissipation between p=3 and p=5;
- sensitivity of thin vortical filaments to their phase and location;
- density-based AMR preferentially resolving the contact while potentially under-resolving some shear-dominated structures.

No exact-method override is warranted. The full problem is two-dimensional, nonlinear, gravitationally forced, wall-bounded, and develops vortical roll-up. Method of characteristics, separation of variables, and linear RT theory cannot provide the complete solution through \(t=3\).

### Targeted Improvements

- **For Integrity:** None. Preserve the successful completion, archival, and positivity checks. Keep the final finite/minimum-\(\rho\)/minimum-\(p\) summaries near the end of the log for auditability.

- **For Accuracy:**  
  Perform a **new p=7 primary run with p=5 as the companion**, changing only polynomial degree. Retain HLLC, Ranocha volume flux, `alpha_max=0.5`, the present AMR limits, and adaptive time stepping. This respects the recommended `polydeg ≤ 7` limit and is preferable to costly uniform refinement. Compare p=3/p=5/p=7 trends in tips, mixing width, kinetic energy, and common-grid field differences. Do not repeat the p=3/p=5-only certification.

- **For Details:**  
  If p=5/p=7 differences remain concentrated in the late-time rolled-up filaments, use **targeted rather than uniform refinement**: augment the density AMR signal with a shear/vorticity measure and, if necessary, permit one additional local AMR level only around the mixing layer. Do not increase shock-capturing dissipation or revert to HLL; there is no visible ringing justifying that trade-off, and it would soften the contact.

- **For Optimality:** None. DGSEM with HLLC, entropy-conservative volume flux, subcell shock capturing, AMR, and adaptive high-order time integration is an appropriate architecture. The remaining requirement is convergence evidence, not another method change.

**Recommendation:** Refine once at p=7 and reassess convergence. Until then, treat Trial #8 as an excellent numerical solution, but not yet as fully certified PINN ground truth.

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report — ATHENA Lab
## 2D Compressible Euler Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (Refinement Trial #10: p=7 Certification Run)

---

**1. Problem Statement**

We solve the inviscid 2D compressible Euler equations with a gravitational source for the single-mode Rayleigh–Taylor instability in the inverted configuration (heavy fluid below light, body force in $+y$). Per the Advisor's directive, this trial is a **pure polynomial-degree refinement**: a **p=7 primary run** with a **p=5 companion**, all other numerics frozen at the Run #9 configuration, to certify convergence of the late-time rolled-up filaments.

---

**1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D Compressible Euler with gravity source ($\gamma = 1.4$):
$$\partial_t \mathbf{U} + \partial_x \mathbf{F}(\mathbf{U}) + \partial_y \mathbf{G}(\mathbf{U}) = \mathbf{S}(\mathbf{U})$$
$$\mathbf{U} = \begin{pmatrix}\rho\\ \rho u\\ \rho v\\ \rho E\end{pmatrix},\quad \mathbf{F} = \begin{pmatrix}\rho u\\ \rho u^2 + p\\ \rho u v\\ u(\rho E + p)\end{pmatrix},\quad \mathbf{G} = \begin{pmatrix}\rho v\\ \rho u v\\ \rho v^2 + p\\ v(\rho E + p)\end{pmatrix},\quad \mathbf{S} = \begin{pmatrix}0\\ 0\\ \rho g\\ \rho g\, v\end{pmatrix}$$
with $g = 1.0$ (positive $y$), equation of state $p = (\gamma-1)\rho e$, and total energy $E = e + \tfrac{1}{2}(u^2 + v^2)$, i.e. $\rho E = \frac{p}{\gamma-1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)$.

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $t \in [0, 3.0]$.

* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x$-direction: Periodic.
  * $y = 0$ and $y = 1$: Reflective / free-slip walls (no-penetration: $v = 0$ at the walls).

* **Initial Conditions** (interface at $y = 0.5$; every field exact):
$$\rho(x,y) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left[1 + \tanh\!\left(50\,(y - 0.5)\right)\right]$$
$$p(x,y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5\\[2pt] y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5\end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y) = 0$$
$$v(x,y) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\,\cos(8\pi x)\,\sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c(x,y) = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(x,y)}{\rho(x,y)}}$$
$$\rho E(x,y) = \frac{p(x,y)}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\rho(x,y)\left(u^2 + v^2\right)$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g} = [0, 1.0]^T$ (source adds momentum in $+y$); $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$; $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$, $\rho_{light} = 1.0$; interface $y = 0.5$; tanh smoothing parameter $50$; perturbation amplitude $0.025$; perturbation wavenumber $8\pi$ (single mode across the domain).

* **Expected Physics:** Linear RT growth, then nonlinear mushroom-cap formation by $t \approx 2$; heavy-fluid spikes penetrate $+y$, light-fluid bubbles penetrate $-y$; secondary Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up filaments on the spike flanks by $t = 3$; mixing-layer width $\approx 0.72$–$0.74$ at $t=3$; monotonically increasing kinetic energy (potential → kinetic conversion; total energy not conserved due to the gravitational source).

---

**2. Strategic Methodology**

* **Can we obtain an exact solution?** **No.** The system is 2D, nonlinear, gravitationally forced, wall-bounded, and develops multi-scale vortical mixing (RT spikes/bubbles + KH roll-up). The Method of Characteristics applies only to 1D simple-wave/Riemann structures; here multi-dimensional wave interactions preclude any implicit characteristic representation of the full field through $t=3$. Separation of variables and linear RT growth-rate theory cover only the early-time regime and cannot deliver the fields. The Advisor explicitly confirms: **no exact-method override is warranted.**
* **Selection:** The one best method is the **Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time**, with subcell shock capturing and AMR, certified by **polynomial-degree convergence (p=7 primary vs. p=5 companion, with archived p=3 trend)**. Since MoC/exact solutions are infeasible, no entropy-condition root selection is required.

---

**3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use "template defaults" **EXCEPT** for the Advisor-mandated adjustments in §3.1 below.

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis:**
  * **AMR variable: $\rho$ (density).** The material interface/contact is the defining structural feature of RTI; density remains sharp and banded *only* at the interface and mixing layer for all $t \in [0,3]$, making it the highest signal-to-noise refinement key. It is superior to velocity, which becomes globally chaotic in the turbulent mixing layer (refining on it wastes elements domain-wide), and to pressure, which is a smooth hydrostatic background weakly informative of the contact.
  * **Shock-capturing indicator: $\rho$** (Hennemann–Gassner), since the dominant discontinuity is the material interface.
  * **Advisor-mandated conditional augmentation:** if p=5/p=7 differences remain concentrated in the late-time rolled-up filaments, augment the density AMR signal with a **shear/vorticity measure restricted to the mixing layer** (density alone may under-resolve shear-dominated KH structures). Do **not** refine globally on velocity.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides — supersede template defaults):**
  1. **Primary run: `polydeg = 7`. Companion run: `polydeg = 5`.** Change **only** the polynomial degree relative to Run #9 (p=5/p=3). Respect the `polydeg ≤ 7` limit. Do **not** switch to uniform refinement.
  2. **Retain** surface flux `flux_hllc`; volume integral `VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG` with `IndicatorHennemannGassner` on density, `alpha_max = 0.5`, `volume_flux_dg = flux_ranocha`, `volume_flux_fv = flux_lax_friedrichs`.
  3. **Retain** AMR: `IndicatorLöhner` on density, `ControllerThreeLevel` (base=4, med=5, max=6), AMR interval = 20.
  4. **Retain** time integration: `RDPK3SpFSAL49` with `StepsizeCallback` (adaptive), initial dt = 1e-5, $t \in [0,3]$.
  5. **Retain** archival of the full conservative state + AMR metadata every `dt_save = 0.1`, and the final integrity log (t=3.0 reached, all-finite check, minimum $\rho$, minimum $p$) printed near the end of stdout for auditability.
  6. **Conditional targeted refinement:** if p=5/p=7 differences persist in the late-time filaments, add the shear/vorticity AMR augmentation and permit **one additional local AMR level (max=7) only around the mixing layer**. Do **not** increase shock-capturing dissipation and do **not** revert to HLL — there is no ringing justifying that trade-off.
  7. **Convergence certification:** report **p=3/p=5/p=7 trends** in spike/bubble tip positions, mixing-layer width, kinetic-energy history, and common-grid (512×2048 AMR-aware, gapless) relative $L_2$ differences of $\rho$ and $v$ at $t = 1, 2, 3$. Reuse the archived Run #9 p=3 (and p=5) snapshots if present; do **not** repeat the p=3/p=5-only certification.

---

**4. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection (Analysis):** Plot **$\rho$ (density)** and **$v$ (vertical velocity)** only. Density is the primary driver and interface marker of RTI (spike/bubble/filament morphology); vertical velocity localizes the plume and mixing-layer dynamics. $u$ and $p$ are secondary and would shrink the panels without adding diagnostic value.
* **Task:** Plot these two fields at **4 distinct times: $t = 0.5,\ 1.0,\ 2.0,\ 3.0$**, taken from the **p=7 primary run**.
* **Layout:** Grid — 2 rows (fields: $\rho$, $v$) × 4 columns (times).
* **Saving Data:** Archive the **full solution state** (all conservative variables + AMR metadata) every `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth. Plotting selection affects only `summary_all.png`.
* **Constraints:**
  * Save as: `summary_all.png` (strictly).
  * **CRITICAL: DISABLE COLORBARS (`colorbar = false`)** — the code is in Julia.
  * Do not use fixed colormaps; let scales adjust automatically per panel.
  * Plot only primary variables ($\rho$, $v = \rho v / \rho$); do not compute derived quantities (e.g., vorticity) in the figure.

---

**5. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**

* **Trigger check:** The [USER_QUESTION] contains **no external data paths** (no sensors, no reference `.npz`). No external loading is mandated.
* **Self-convergence metrics (mandatory, printed to stdout):** relative $L_2$ differences of $\rho$ and $v$ between p=7 and p=5 (and archived p=3) on the common 512×2048 grid at $t = 1, 2, 3$; spike/bubble tip $y$-positions; mixing-layer widths; kinetic-energy histories. These are resolution-difference diagnostics, not errors against an exact solution, and must be labeled as such.

- New Code (lines 53-105):
```julia
# A reusable archive must contain the complete 0.0:dt_save:3.0 history and
# all fields required for AMR-aware reconstruction and kinetic-energy audits.
function complete_npz_archive(directory, expected_polydeg)
    isdir(directory) || return false
    files = sort([joinpath(directory, file) for file in readdir(directory)
                  if endswith(file, ".npz")])
    isempty(files) && return false

    required_keys = ("t", "polydeg", "u_cons", "leaf_node_coordinates",
                     "leaf_element_bounds", "domain_min", "domain_max")
    times = Float64[]

    for file in files
        data = npzread(file)
        all(key -> haskey(data, key), required_keys) || return false

        time_value = data["t"]
        degree_value = data["polydeg"]
        time = Float64(time_value isa Number ? time_value : only(time_value))
        degree = Int(degree_value isa Number ? degree_value : only(degree_value))
        degree == expected_polydeg || return false
        push!(times, time)
    end

    required_times = collect(0.0:dt_save:3.0)
    push!(required_times, 3.0) # Explicitly require the final integrity snapshot.
    return all(target_time ->
               any(time -> isapprox(time, target_time;
                                    atol=1.0e-6, rtol=0.0), times),
               required_times)
end

println("Starting p=7 primary RTI run...")
sol = solve(ode, RDPK3SpFSAL49();
            dt=1.0e-5, callback=callbacks, adaptive=false);

# Preserve explicit primary-run handles for certification and post-processing.
polydeg_p7 = polydeg
semi_p7 = semi
ode_p7 = ode
sol_p7 = sol
checks_p7 = log_run_checks("p=7 primary", sol_p7, semi_p7)

npz_out_p5 = joinpath(npz_root, "p5")
npz_out_p3 = joinpath(npz_root, "p3")
p5_archive_reused = complete_npz_archive(npz_out_p5, 5)
p3_archive_available = complete_npz_archive(npz_out_p3, 3)

# The p=5 companion is run only when a complete Run #9 archive is unavailable.
polydeg_p5 = 5
sol_p5 = nothing
semi_p5 = nothing
ode_p5 = nothing
checks_p5 = nothing

if p5_archive_reused
    println("Reusing complete archived Run #9 p=5 companion snapshots from ",
            npz_out_p5)
else
    # Companion p=5 run: identical physics, fluxes, AMR, and time-step control.
    basis_p5 = LobattoLegendreBasis(polydeg_p5)
    indicator_sc_p5 = IndicatorHennemannGassner(equations, basis_p5,
                                                alpha_max=0.5,
                                                alpha_min=0.001,
                                                alpha_smooth=true,
                                                variable=density_indicator)
    volume_integral_p5 = VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG(
        indicator_sc_p5;
        volume_flux_dg=flux_ranocha,
        volume_flux_fv=flux_lax_friedrichs)
    dg_p5 = DGSEM(polydeg=polydeg_p5,
                  surface_flux=flux_hllc,
                  volume_integral=volume_integral_p5)

    mesh_p5 = P4estMesh((1, 1), polydeg=polydeg_p5,
                        coordinates_min=coordinates_min,
                        coordinates_max=coordinates_max,
                        periodicity=(true, false))
    semi_p5 = SemidiscretizationHyperbolic(mesh_p5, equations,
                                           initial_condition_rti, dg_p5;
                                           boundary_conditions=boundary_conditions,
                                           source_terms=source_terms_gravity)

    amr_indicator_p5 = IndicatorLöhner(semi_p5, variable=density_indicator)
    amr_controller_p5 = ControllerThreeLevel(semi_p5, amr_indicator_p5,
                                             base_level=4,
                                             med_level=5, med_threshold=0.1,
                                             max_level=6, max_threshold=0.3)
    amr_callback_p5 = AMRCallback(semi_p5, amr_controller_p5,
                                  interval=20,
                                  adapt_initial_condition=true)

    ode_p5 = semidiscretize(semi_p5, tspan)
    callbacks_p5 = make_run_callbacks(semi_p5, amr_callback_p5, polydeg_p5)

    println("Starting p=5 companion verification run...")
    sol_p5 = solve(ode_p5, RDPK3SpFSAL49();
                   dt=1.0e-5, callback=callbacks_p5, adaptive=false);
    checks_p5 = log_run_checks("p=5 companion", sol_p5, semi_p5)
end

if p3_archive_available
    println("Reusing complete archived Run #9 p=3 snapshots from ", npz_out_p3)
else
    println("Complete archived Run #9 p=3 snapshots are unavailable; ",
            "p=3 will not be rerun and its trend will be omitted.")
end
```

```julia
function print_grid_metrics(label, grid)
    metrics = interface_metrics(grid)
    kinetic_energy = uniform_grid_kinetic_energy(grid)
    Printf.@printf(
        "  %s: bubble tip y = %.8f, spike tip y = %.8f, mixing width = %.8f, KE = %.12e\n",
        label, metrics.bubble_tip, metrics.spike_tip,
        metrics.mixing_width, kinetic_energy)
    return nothing
end

println("\nCommon-grid polynomial-degree certification on the 512 x 2048 grid.")
println("These are resolution-difference diagnostics, not errors against an exact solution.")
println("The new p=7 run is the comparison reference, not certified exact ground truth.")

for target_time in (1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
    primary_index = findfirst(
        time -> isapprox(time, target_time; atol=1.0e-6, rtol=0.0),
        target_times)
    primary_index === nothing &&
        error("Primary p=7 grid is missing t = $target_time.")

    grid_p7 = primary_grids[primary_index]
    grid_p5 = interpolate_amr_snapshot(
        snapshot_at_time(npz_out_p5, target_time))
    grid_p3 = p3_archive_available ?
              interpolate_amr_snapshot(
                  snapshot_at_time(npz_out_p3, target_time)) : nothing

    Printf.@printf("\nt = %.1f\n", target_time)
    if grid_p3 !== nothing
        print_grid_metrics("p=3", grid_p3)
    end
    print_grid_metrics("p=5", grid_p5)
    print_grid_metrics("p=7", grid_p7)

    l2_rho_p5 = relative_l2_difference(grid_p5.rho, grid_p7.rho)
    l2_v_p5 = relative_l2_difference(grid_p5.v, grid_p7.v)
    Printf.@printf(
        "  p=5 versus p=7 relative L2 resolution differences: rho = %.8e, v = %.8e\n",
        l2_rho_p5, l2_v_p5)

    if grid_p3 !== nothing
        l2_rho_p3 = relative_l2_difference(grid_p3.rho, grid_p7.rho)
        l2_v_p3 = relative_l2_difference(grid_p3.v, grid_p7.v)
        Printf.@printf(
            "  p=3 versus p=7 relative L2 resolution differences: rho = %.8e, v = %.8e\n",
            l2_rho_p3, l2_v_p3)
    end
end

# Audit final archived states so reused Run #9 companions are also explicitly
# checked near the end of stdout.
function log_archive_checks(label, directory)
    data = snapshot_at_time(directory, 3.0)
    final_time = snapshot_time(data)
    reached_t_end = isapprox(final_time, 3.0; atol=1.0e-10, rtol=0.0)

    u_cons = data["u_cons"]
    fields_finite = all(isfinite, u_cons)
    min_rho = Inf
    min_pressure = Inf
    gamma = equations.gamma

    for element in axes(u_cons, 4), j in axes(u_cons, 3), i in axes(u_cons, 2)
        rho    = u_cons[1, i, j, element]
        rho_v1 = u_cons[2, i, j, element]
        rho_v2 = u_cons[3, i, j, element]
        rho_e  = u_cons[4, i, j, element]

        pressure = (gamma - 1) *
                   (rho_e - 0.5 * (rho_v1^2 + rho_v2^2) / rho)
        min_rho = min(min_rho, rho)
        min_pressure = min(min_pressure, pressure)
    end

    println("[$label] successful arrival at t = 3.0: ", reached_t_end,
            " (final archived t = ", final_time, ")")
    println("[$label] all fields finite (no NaN/Inf): ", fields_finite)
    println("[$label] minimum rho = ", min_rho,
            "; min rho > 0: ", min_rho > 0)
    println("[$label] minimum p = ", min_pressure,
            "; min p > 0: ", min_pressure > 0)
    return nothing
end

println("\nFinal integrity and positivity audit:")
for (label, directory) in archive_runs
    log_archive_checks(label, directory)
end
println("Done.")
```

--- 4. Calling Debugging Team ---
Inspector summary:
 The code implements a Trixi-based 2D compressible Euler RTI solver with DGSEM. It uses gamma=1.4, domain [0,0.25]x[0,1], periodic x, slip walls in y, the specified exact density/pressure/velocity initial conditions, and the +y gravity source. The primary run uses polydeg=7; a p=5 companion is reused if a complete archive exists, otherwise it is run. Shock capturing uses IndicatorHennemannGassner on density with alpha_max=0.5, VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG with flux_ranocha/flux_lax_friedrichs, and surface flux flux_hllc. AMR uses IndicatorLöhner on density with ControllerThreeLevel base=4/med=5/max=6 and interval=20. Time integration uses RDPK3SpFSAL49 with StepsizeCallback, initial dt=1e-5, and tspan=(0,3). The code archives NPZ snapshots every dt_save=0.1, saves summary_all.png with rho and v at t=0.5,1,2,3, prints convergence diagnostics on a 512x2048 common grid, and performs final positivity/finiteness audits.
Report: There are discrepancies with the base strategy:
The implementation does not include the Advisor-mandated conditional shear/vorticity-aware targeted AMR augmentation restricted to the mixing layer, nor the conditional local max=7 refinement. It only implements density-keyed Löhner AMR with max=6. The code also does not implement any trigger that checks whether p=5/p=7 differences remain concentrated in late-time rolled-up filaments and then augments refinement accordingly. In addition, the p=3 convergence trend is only reported if a complete archived p=3 dataset is already present; if that archive is missing, the required p=3/p=5/p=7 certification trend is not produced.

Cell 7: Dynamic Adaptive Mesh Refinement (AMR) Strategy
- Action: Preserve the baseline density-keyed Löhner indicator, three-level controller with levels 4/5/6, and AMR interval 20.
